H_{\infty}\in[0,H_{0}]
and also item
1
. The convergence of
H_{n}
-multi-graphs
is item
2
is also standard, as they have uniform curvature estimates
coming from stability. Item
3
is also standard by an
induction argument in
k^{\prime}
and a diagonal argument. Items
4
,

5
and
6
follow from an adaptation of the proof
Proposition
5.13
.
∎

Corollary 6.2
.

Given
I\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
\tau\in(0,\pi/10]
,
let
\Lambda=\Lambda(I,H_{0},{\varepsilon}_{0},A_{0},K_{0})
be as given in Definition
1.1
.
Let
F_{n}\colon M_{n}\looparrowright X
be a sequence of
H_{n}
-immersions
in
\Lambda
where all of the
M_{n}
are connected and
X
is compact.
Then, given base points
q_{n}\in M_{n}
, a subsequence of the
F_{n}

converges to a branched
H
-immersion

F_{\infty}\colon\Sigma\looparrowright X
of index at most
I
,
where the convergence as
n\to\infty

takes place in the intrinsic balls
B_{M_{n}}(q(n),i)
,
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
and this convergence is described in Theorem
6.1
.

Remark 6.3
.

Consider a sequence
F_{n}\colon M_{n}\looparrowright X
of complete
H_{n}
-immersions
in the space
\Lambda
as described in the statement of Theorem
6.1
,
with limit branched
H_{\infty}
-immersion

F_{\infty}\colon\Sigma\looparrowright X
described in item 6 of the theorem.

1.

If
F_{\infty}
has a branch point at some
q\in\Sigma
of branching order

l\in\mathbb{N}
, then item 6(b) implies

q\in\cup_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{\Sigma_{i}}}\mathcal{B}_{\Sigma_{i}}\subset\cup_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}\Sigma_{i}
.
The proof of the theorem gives that there are blow-up points

q(n)\in M_{n}
that yield, under blowing-up, a
limit complete, possibly finitely branched minimal surface
M
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}

with finite total curvature and such that one of the ends
E
of
M

has multiplicity
l+1
; such an end is not embedded, and there are
portions of the
F_{n}
converging to
E
which fail to be injective.
Hence, the existence of branch points for the limit branched
immersion
F_{\infty}
implies that for
n
large,
the sequence
F_{n}
restricted to
\cup_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}\Delta_{i}(n)

is not injective. In particular if
F_{n}
is
injective for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then any limit
F_{\infty}\colon\Sigma\looparrowright X

given by the theorem has no branch points.

2.

Assume
F_{\infty}
has at least one branch point. By item
5

of Theorem
6.1
, every branch point
b
of
F_{\infty}
lies
in some set
\mathcal{B}_{\Sigma_{i}}
for some
i\in\mathcal{U}
, and the branch order of
F_{\infty}

at
b
is equal to
S_{\infty}(b)-1
. Adding this along the set
\mathcal{B}_{F_{\infty}}

of branch points of
F_{\infty}
, we get that the total branching order of
F_{\infty}

is at most

\sum_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{F_{\infty}}}[S_{\infty}(b)-1]\leq 3I-1.

7
Appendix A: Curvature estimates for stable
H
-surfaces

Rosenberg, Toubiana and Souam
[
25
, Main Theorem]

proved that there exists a universal constant

C^{\prime}_{s}>0
such that for any
K_{0}\geq 0
and any complete Riemannian
3-manifold
(Y,g)
of absolute sectional curvature at most
K_{0}
, every
stable two-sided
H
-immersion
F\colon M\looparrowright Y
in satisfies

|A_{M}|(p)\leq\frac{C^{\prime}_{s}}{\min\{d_{M}(p,\partial M),\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{K_{0}}}\}}.

(7.1)

Observe that the above curvature estimate fails to hold when the

H
-immersion is minimal and one-sided; a counterexample can be
constructed whenever a complete flat three-manifold
Y
admits a
complete, non-totally geodesic,
stable one-sided minimal surface without boundary, see Remark
7.2
for examples.
The next theorem is an adaptation of (
7.1
)
that includes curvature estimates for the case of one-sided minimal surfaces in
Y
,
see also Corollaries 9 and 10 in
[
24
]
.

Theorem 7.1

(Curvature estimate for stable
H
-surfaces)
.

There exists
C^{\prime\prime}_{s}\geq 2\pi
such that given
K_{0}>0
and
a complete Riemannian 3-manifold
(Y,g)
of bounded sectional curvature

|K|\leq K_{0}
, then for any connected, immersed,
one-sided, stable minimal surface
M\looparrowright Y
and for any
p\in M
,

|A_{M}|(p)\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime}_{s}}{\min\{\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y}(p),d_{M}(p,\partial M),\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{K_{0}}}\}}.

(7.2)

Let
C_{s}:=\max\{C^{\prime}_{s},C^{\prime\prime}_{s}\}
, where
C^{\prime}_{s}
is defined by
(
7.1
). Given
{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
,
K_{0}\geq 0
, if
X
is a
complete Riemannian 3-manifold with injectivity radius at
least
{\varepsilon}_{0}
and bounded sectional curvature
|K|\leq K_{0}
,
and
F\colon M\looparrowright X
is a stable
H
-immersion,
then

|A_{M}|(p)\leq\frac{C_{s}}{\min\{{\varepsilon}_{0},d_{M}(p,\partial M),\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{K_{0}}}\}}.

(7.3)

Proof.

Clearly the validity of (
7.2
) implies that
(
7.3
) holds. Also observe that by
Remark
7.2
, any
C^{\prime\prime}_{s}>0
that satisfies
(
7.2
) must be at least
2\pi
; in particular,
C_{s}\geq 2\pi

(in fact,
C_{s}\geq C_{s}^{\prime}>2\pi
, see Remark
7.2
).

We next prove the existence of a universal constant
C_{s}^{\prime\prime}

satisfying (
7.2
) by contradiction. Since
(
7.2
) is invariant under re-scaling, by
scaling the ambient Riemannian metric by

\frac{\sqrt{K_{0}}}{\pi}
we may assume that there exists a sequence

\{M_{n}\looparrowright Y_{n}\}_{n}
of one-sided, stable
minimal surfaces with boundary, immersed in complete
Riemannian 3-manifolds
(Y_{n},g_{n})
with absolute
sectional curvature
|K_{Y_{n}}|\leq\pi^{2}
, and points

p_{n}\in M_{n}
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

|A_{M_{n}}|(p_{n})\cdot\min\{\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}),d_{M_{n}}(p_{n},\partial M_{n}),1/2\}\geq n.

(7.4)

Consider the open geodesic disk
D_{n}\subset M_{n}
of center

p_{n}
and radius
d_{M_{n}}(p_{n},\partial M_{n})
. Let

p_{n}^{*}\in D_{n}
be a maximum of the continuous function

f_{n}\colon D_{n}\to\mathbb{R},\quad f_{n}(x)=|A_{M_{n}}|(x)\cdot\min\{\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(x),d_{D_{n}}(x,\partial D_{n}),1/2\}.

After passing to a subsequence, we can assume that one of
the following three cases occurs for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(A)

\min\{\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*}),d_{D_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},\partial D_{n}),1/2\}=\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*})
.

(B)

\min\{\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*}),d_{D_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},\partial D_{n}),1/2\}=d_{D_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},\partial D_{n})
.

(C)

\min\{\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*}),d_{D_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},\partial D_{n}),1/2\}=1/2
.

Suppose that case (C) holds. Since
\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*})\geq 1/2
, then Lemma 2.2 in
[
25
]
implies:

The injectivity radius function of

B_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/2)
restricted to
B_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/8)

is at least
1/8
.

(7.5)

Applying Theorem 2.1 in
[
25
]
to the choices

M=B_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/2)
,
\Lambda=\pi^{2}
,
\Omega=B_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/10)
,
\Omega({\delta})=B_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/8)
,

i=1/8
, we conclude that every point
x\in B_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/10)
admits harmonic coordinates centered
at
x
and defined on the geodesic ball
B_{Y_{n}}(x,{\varepsilon}_{0})

for some
{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
independent of
x
and
n
, and the metric

g_{n}
is
C^{1,{\alpha}}
-controlled in the sense of
Definition
2.2
in terms of a constant
Q>1
which
is also independent of
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Let
{\lambda}_{n}=|A_{M_{n}}|(p_{n}^{*})
, which tends to
\infty
as

n\to\infty
because

\frac{1}{2}|A_{M_{n}}|(p_{n}^{*})=f_{n}(p_{n}^{*})\geq f_{n}(p_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:thm6.6a})}}{{\geq}}n.

(7.6)

Define
B_{n}^{\prime}=\left(B_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/10),{\lambda}_{n}^{2}\,g_{n}\right)
. The sequence of
3
-manifolds
\{B_{n}^{\prime}\}_{n}
converges
C^{1,{\alpha}}

to
\mathbb{R}^{3}
with its standard metric and
the harmonic coordinates in
B^{\prime}_{n}
centered at
p_{n}^{*}

converge as
n\to\infty
to the usual harmonic coordinates
centered at the origin.

Consider the sequence of immersed, one-sided, stable minimal
surfaces

\Delta_{n}=\left(B_{M_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/10),{\lambda}_{n}^{2}\,g_{n}\right)\looparrowright B^{\prime}_{n}.

Observe that the intrinsic distances in
\Delta_{n}
from

p_{n}^{*}
to the boundary of
\Delta_{n}
diverge to

\infty
. We claim that the
\Delta_{n}
have uniformly
bounded second fundamental form: Take
x\in B_{M_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/10)
. Since
x\in D_{n}
because we are in
case (C), and thus

|A_{M_{n}}|(x)\cdot\min\{\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(x),d_{D_{n}}(x,\partial D_{n}),1/2\}=f_{n}(x)\leq f_{n}(p_{n}^{*})=\frac{{\lambda}_{n}}{2},

or equivalently,

|A_{\Delta_{n}}|(x)\cdot\min\{\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(x),d_{D_{n}}(x,\partial D_{n}),1/2\}\leq 1/2.

(7.7)

Observe that
\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(x)\geq 1/8
by
(
7.5
). Also,
d_{D_{n}}(x,\partial D_{n})\geq 2/5

because
x\in B_{M_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/10)
,

B_{M_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},1/2)\subset D_{n}
and by the triangle
inequality. Hence, the minimum in the LHS of
(
7.7
) is at least
1/8
, from where we deduce
that
|A_{\Delta_{n}}|(x)\leq 4
, and our claim is proved.

Therefore, after passing to a subsequence, the
\Delta_{n}

converge to a complete minimal surface
S
immersed in

\mathbb{R}^{3}
with bounded second fundamental form, see the
arguments at the beginning of Section
5.4

for details.

We claim that
S
is stable. If
S
is two-sided, this is
standard, see e.g.
[
25
, page 636]
. We next give a
different argument that is valid regardless of whether
S

is one- or two-sided. Stability of
S
in the one-sided
case amounts to show that

\int_{\widetilde{S}}|A_{\widetilde{S}}|^{2}\phi^{2}\leq\int_{\widetilde{S}}|\nabla\phi|^{2},

(7.8)

for every compactly supported smooth function
\phi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\widetilde{S})
defined on the two-sided cover

\widetilde{S}
of
S
that is anti-invariant, see
Definition
2.1
. Given such a function
\phi
,
we can view
\phi
for
n
sufficiently large as a
compactly supported smooth function
\phi_{n}
defined on the
two-sided cover
\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}
of
\Delta_{n}

that is anti-invariant, and thus, by stability of

\Delta_{n}
, we have

\int_{\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}}(|A_{\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}}|^{2}+\mbox{Ric}_{B^{\prime}_{n}}(N_{n},N_{n}))\phi_{n}^{2}\leq\int_{\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}}|\nabla\phi_{n}|^{2},

(7.9)

where Ric
{}_{B^{\prime}_{n}}
denotes the Ricci curvature of
B^{\prime}_{n}

and
N_{n}
is a unit normal vector to
\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}
in

B^{\prime}_{n}
. The
C^{1,{\alpha}}
convergence of the metrics
{\lambda}_{n}\,g_{n}
to the flat metric on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
allows us to take limits
in (
7.9
) as
n\to\infty
to obtain
(
7.8
), and thus,
S
is stable.

The desired contradiction (that proves (
7.2
)
in the case that (C) holds) comes from the fact that there
are no complete stable non-flat minimal surfaces in

\mathbb{R}^{3}
, see Ros
[
24
, Theorem 8]
.

Next we will explain how to reduce case (A) to case (C).
If case (A) holds, we have
\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*})\leq 1/2
.
Let
\mu_{n}=1/\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*})
. Define
Y_{n}^{\prime}=\left(Y_{n},\mu_{n}^{2}\,g_{n}\right)
and
M^{\prime}_{n}=(M_{n},\mu_{n}^{2}\,g_{n})
.
Note that
\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y^{\prime}_{n}}(p_{n}^{*})=1
, the absolute sectional
curvature of
Y^{\prime}_{n}
is less than or equal to
\frac{\pi^{2}}{\mu_{n}^{2}}\leq\pi^{2}
, which implies we may use the upper
estimate
K_{0}=\pi^{2}
(in other words,
(M^{\prime}_{n},Y_{n}^{\prime})
is a
possible counterexample to (
7.2
) under the
normalization introduced in the second paragraph of this
proof), and so
\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{K_{0}}}=1/2
. Observe
that
(M^{\prime}_{n},Y^{\prime}_{n})
lies in case (C) because
d_{M^{\prime}_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},\partial M^{\prime}_{n})=\mu_{n}\,d_{M_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},\partial M_{n})\geq 1
, and so,
\min\{\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y^{\prime}_{n}}(p_{n}^{*}),d_{M^{\prime}_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},\partial M^{\prime}_{n}),1/2)\}=1/2
. If we check that

|A_{M^{\prime}_{n}}|(p_{n}^{*})\cdot\min\{\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y^{\prime}_{n}}(p_{n}^{*}),d_{M^{\prime}_{n}}(p_{n}^{*},\partial M^{\prime}_{n}),1/2)\}\to\infty

(7.10)

then we will find a contradiction as we did in case (C).
To see this, observe that two times the LHS of
(
7.10
) can be written as

|A_{M^{\prime}_{n}}|(p_{n}^{*})=|A_{M^{\prime}_{n}}|(p_{n}^{*})\cdot\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y^{\prime}_{n}}(p_{n}^{*})=|A_{M_{n}}|(p_{n}^{*})\cdot\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y_{n}}(p_{n}^{*})=f_{n}(p_{n}^{*})\geq n\to\infty,

which finishes the proof in case (A) occurs.
Similar reasoning reduces case (B) to case (C), which completes
the proof of Theorem
7.1
.
∎

Remark 7.2

(Lower bound estimates for
C^{\prime}_{s}
and
C_{s}^{\prime\prime}
)
.

We claim that
\pi
,
2\pi
are lower bounds for
C^{\prime}_{s}
,

C^{\prime\prime}_{s}
, respectively. To see this, consider the Scherk’s doubly periodic
minimal surface
M({\theta})
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
{\theta}\in(0,\pi/2]
, and
its non-orientable, embedded quotient surface
\widehat{M}({\theta})

with total curvature
-2\pi
in the flat quotient manifold

Y({\theta})=T_{\theta}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
where
T_{\theta}=\mathbb{R}^{2}/\mbox{Span}\{w_{1}({\theta}),w_{2}({\theta})\}
, where

w_{1}({\theta})=\frac{\pi}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\cos({\theta}/2)},0,0\right),\quad w_{2}({\theta})=\frac{\pi}{2}\left(0,\frac{1}{\sin({\theta}/2)},0\right).

Here, the oriented cover
\widetilde{M}({\theta})
of
\widehat{M}({\theta})
is
conformally
(\mathbb{C}\cup\{\infty\})\setminus\{e^{\pm i{\theta}/2}\}

with Weierstrass data

\left(g(z)=z,\quad\omega=\frac{i\,dz}{\Pi(z\pm e^{\pm i{\theta}/2})}\right).

Straightforward calculations show that at
z=0
in

(\mathbb{C}\cup\{\infty\})\setminus\{e^{\pm i\pi/4}\}
viewed as a point
of
\widehat{M}({\theta})
, the absolute Gaussian curvature is given
by
|K|(0)=16
and this point is the unique maximum of

|K|
on
\widehat{M}({\theta})
. On the other hand, the injectivity
radius of
Y({\theta})
(at every point) equals
\frac{\pi}{4\cos({\theta}/2)}
which has a maximum value of
\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{2}}

at
{\theta}=\pi/2
. Therefore, for any
\theta\in/0,\pi/2]
we have

|A_{\widehat{M}({\theta})}|\cdot\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y({\theta})}\leq|A_{\widehat{M}(\pi/2)}|(0)\cdot\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y(\pi/2)}=|4\sqrt{2}|\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{2}}=2\pi.

Hence the constant
C^{\prime\prime}_{s}
in the above theorem must be at least
2\pi
.

The standard fundamental region
Q
for
\widehat{M}(\pi/4)
in

\mathbb{R}^{3}
is a vertical graph bounded by 4
vertical lines and

|A_{Q}|(0)\cdot d_{Q}(0,\partial Q)=4\sqrt{2}\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{2}}=2\pi,

so the constant
C^{\prime}_{s}
in (
7.1
) also
must be at least
2\pi
. In fact,
C^{\prime}_{s}
can be seen to be strictly
greater than
2\pi
by consideration of the intersection of

M(\theta)
with a ball of radius slightly larger than

\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{2}}
. Therefore,
the constant
C_{s}
given in given in the above
theorem also must be greater than
2\pi
.

Next consider the translational quotient of
H
of a
helicoid in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
H
is an embedded, one-sided,
stable minimal surface in
Y=\mathbb{R}^{3}/(\pi\mathbb{Z})
with finite
total curvature
-2\pi
. Let
p\in H
be any point on the
axis of
H
. Then,

|A_{H}|\cdot\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y}\leq|A_{H}|(p)|\cdot\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y}(p)=|\sqrt{2}|\frac{\pi}{2}=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{2}}.

The slab-type region
W
of
H
bounded by
two straight lines inside
H
at distance
\pi
apart is
stable, and the function
p\in W\mapsto|A_{W}|(p)d_{W}(p,\partial W)
has a maximum value at the
mid point of the segment obtained by intersecting the axis
of
H
with
W
, hence

|A_{W}|(p)\cdot d_{W}(p,\partial W)\leq|A_{W}|(0)\cdot d_{W}(0,\partial W)=|\sqrt{2}|\frac{\pi}{2}=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{2}}.

The above curvature estimates for
\widehat{M}(\pi/2)
,
Q
,

H
and
W
lead us to ask the following question.

Question 7.3
.

If
M
is a complete, one-sided, stable
minimal surface in a complete flat 3-manifold
Y
,
does the following inequality hold?

\mbox{For all }p\in M,\quad|A_{M}|(p)|\cdot\mbox{\rm Inj}_{Y}(p)\leq 2\pi.

More generally, does setting
C_{s}^{\prime\prime}=2\pi
work in Theorem
7.1
?

These questions are also motivated by the result by
Ros
[
24
]
that the only complete non-flat stable
minimal surface in a quotient of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
by a rank one
(resp. two) group of translations is a quotient of the
Helicoid (resp. quotients of the Scherk doubly periodic
minimal surfaces) with total curvature
-2\pi
.

8
Appendix B: Some results in reference
[
17
]
used in this paper

In this section we state, for the readers convenience, some results
from
[
17
]
that we frequently apply in the proofs of the
present paper.

Proposition 8.1

(Intrinsic monotonicity of area formula, Proposition 2.4 in
[
17
]
)
.

Let
\overline{B}_{X}(x_{0},R_{1})
denote a closed geodesic ball in an

m
-dimensional manifold
(X,g)
, where
0<R_{1}\leq\mbox{\rm Inj}_{X}(x_{0})
,
and suppose that
K_{\mbox{\rm\footnotesize sec}}\leq a
on
B_{X}(x_{0},R_{1})

for some
a\in\mathbb{R}
.
Given
H_{0}\geq 0
, define

R_{0}(a,H_{0})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\frac{1}{\sqrt{a}}\mbox{\rm arc cot}\left(\frac{H_{0}}{\sqrt{a}}\right)&\mbox{if }a>0,\\
1/H_{0}&\mbox{if }a=0\quad\mbox{(if $H_{0}=0$ we take $R_{0}(0,0)=\infty$)}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{-a}}\mbox{\rm arc coth}\left(\frac{H_{0}}{\sqrt{-a}}\right),&\mbox{if }a<0\quad\mbox{(if $\frac{H_{0}}{\sqrt{-a}}\geq 1$ we take $R_{0}(a,H_{0})=\infty$),}\end{array}\right.

(8.1)

and let
r_{1}=r_{1}(R_{1},a,H_{0})=\min\{R_{1},R_{0}(a,H_{0})\}
.

Suppose
M
is a complete, immersed, connected
n
-dimensional submanifold of
X

and
x_{0}\in M
is a point such that when
\partial M\neq\varnothing
,

d_{M}(x_{0},\partial M)\geq R_{1}
and the length of the mean curvature vector
\vec{H}
of
M

restricted to
\overline{B}_{X}(x_{0},R_{1})
is bounded from above by
H_{0}
.
Then:

1.

If
M
is compact without boundary, then there exists
y\in M

such that the extrinsic distance from
x_{0}
to
y
is greater than or equal to
r_{1}
.

2.

The
n
-dimensional volume
A(r)
of
B_{M}(x_{0},r)
is
a strictly increasing function of
r\in(0,r_{1}]
.

3.

For all
r\in(0,r_{1}]
when
r_{1}\neq\infty
or otherwise, for all
r\in(0,\infty)
:

A(r)\geq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\omega_{n}\,r^{n}e^{-nH_{0}r}&\mbox{ if $a\leq 0$},\\
\omega_{n}\,r^{n}e^{-nr(H_{0}+\frac{1}{2}f_{a}(r_{1})r)}&\mbox{ if $a>0$},\end{array}\right.

(8.2)

where
\omega_{n}
is the volume of the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
given
a>0
, the function
f_{a}\colon[0,\pi/\sqrt{a})\to\mathbb{R}
is defined
by
f_{a}(t)=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\left[1-t\sqrt{a}\cot(\sqrt{a}t)\right]
,
t\in[0,\pi/\sqrt{a})
.

Corollary 8.2

(Corollary 2.6 in
[
17
]
)
.

Let
R_{1}>0
,
a\in\mathbb{R}
and
H_{0}\geq 0
, and suppose that
X

is a complete Riemannian
m
-dimensional manifold with
injectivity radius at least
R_{1}>0
and
K_{sec}\leq a
.
If
M\looparrowright X
is a complete, non-compact immersed

n
-dimensional submanifold with empty boundary and the mean curvature
vector
\vec{H}
of
M
satisfies
|\vec{H}|\leq H_{0}
, then
M
has infinite
volume.

Proposition 8.3

(Proposition 2.7 in
[
17
]
)
.

Given
R_{1}>0
,
a\in\mathbb{R}
and
H_{0}\geq 0
, there exists

r_{2}=r_{2}(R_{1},a,H_{0})\in(0,r_{1}]

(here
r_{1}
is given by Proposition
8.1
)
such that if
X
is a complete Riemannian 3-manifold with
injectivity radius at least
R_{1}>0
and

K_{sec}\leq a
, and if
M\looparrowright X
is
a complete, connected immersed surface with boundary,
whose mean curvature vector
\vec{H}
satisfies

|\vec{H}|\leq H_{0}
, then for all
p\in\mbox{\rm Int}(M)
we have

\mathrm{Area}[B_{M}(p,r)]\geq 3r^{2},\quad\mbox{whenever $0<r\leq\min\{r_{2},d_{M}(p,\partial M)\}$.}

(8.3)

Furthermore, given
{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
define
\displaystyle C_{A}=\min\{{\varepsilon}_{0},\frac{r_{2}^{2}}{{\varepsilon}_{0}}\}
. If
p\in M
satisfies

d_{M}(p,\partial M)\geq{\varepsilon}_{0}
, then

\mathrm{Area}[B_{M}(p,d_{M}(p,\partial M))]\geq C_{A}\,d_{M}(p,\partial M)

(8.4)

and

\mathrm{Area}[B_{M}(p,{\varepsilon}_{0})]\geq C_{A}\,{\varepsilon}_{0},

(8.5)

We finish this summary of auxiliary results taken
from
[
17
]
with the following scale-invariant weak chord-arc type
estimates for branched minimal surfaces of finite index in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Proposition 8.4

(Proposition 4.1 in
[
17
]
)
.

Given
I,B\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
, let
f\colon(\Sigma,p_{0})\looparrowright(\mathbb{R}^{3},\vec{0})

be a complete, connected, pointed branched
minimal surface with index at most
I
and total branching order at most
B
.
Given
R>0
, let
\Omega_{R}
denote the component
of
f^{-1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}}(R))
that contains
p_{0}
. Then, the following
scale-invariant estimates hold and depend only on
I
,
B
:

1.

For any
p\in\Omega_{R}
,

d_{\Omega_{R}}(p,\partial\Omega_{R})<\widehat{L}R,

(8.6)

where
\widehat{L}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(3I+2B+3)}
.

2.

If
f
is injective with image a plane, then the
distance between any two points of
\Omega_{R}
is less than
or equal to
2R
. Otherwise, given points
p,q
in
\Omega_{R}
,

d_{\Omega_{2R}}(p,q)<\widehat{C}R,

(8.7)

where
\widehat{C}=\widehat{C}(I,B)=8\widehat{L}^{3}+2\pi\widehat{L}^{2}-20\widehat{L}-\frac{\pi}{2}
. In particular,

\Omega_{R}\subset B_{\Sigma}(p,\widehat{C}R)
for every
p\in\Omega_{R}
.
